# Source translation ledger

- Status: Phase 0 seed
- Date: July 23, 2026
- Authority: execution record under [Source translation discipline](source-translation.md)

## Use

This ledger accounts for source ownership and disposition. It is separate from the functional-completeness ledger, which accounts for product capabilities.

The active packet, checkpoint, lane, owner, blocker, and next work live only in the [current implementation plan](current-plan.md). This ledger records source evidence and row state; do not turn it into a competing task queue or current-state diary.

Seed likely source owners as provisional hypotheses. Before an island boundary is fixed, run the bounded [fog-of-war preflight](source-translation.md#fog-of-war-preflight), record the inspected entry points and source or code areas in the affected row text or change record, and add every dependency the pass exposes. Continue tracing real include, call, data, and editor paths while the island is working. Before it closes, replace provisional notes with exact relevant symbols, tests or captures, deliberate differences, native files, and final dispositions.

Each new island also declares a bounded source census under the accepted [source-census rule](source-translation.md#bounded-source-census-and-closure): pinned source revision, observable contract, runtime and editor roots, applicable build variants, native owners, forward semantic dependencies, bounded reverse callers and registrations for entries and owned or mutated state, and any actually present indirect edges. Do not recursively reverse-trace unrelated callers of every shared helper. Every discovered file and relevant symbol or range maps to a row here, and every outward edge is included, evidenced as irrelevant, excluded, or deferred to a named phase and dependency. The independent source-fidelity reviewer reconstructs that census from the declared roots rather than accepting the implementer's row list as proof of discovery.

Persist each census as a compact block in this ledger, or link to one named checked-in change record. Record the revision, observable claim, roots, applicable variants, native owners, sorted file/range row anchors, deferred outward edges, and independent-review result. This block indexes the dispositions below; it is not a task plan, duplicate ledger, or generated graph.

No `discovery complete` column or graph identifier is added: a bounded pass cannot prove that no edge remains. A deterministic generated relationship view may support a row only with recorded source, tool, configuration, and output provenance. It does not replace a source-verified relationship, disposition, observable contract, or the manual accounting required at closure.

Allowed dispositions are translate, platform replacement, import only, superseded mechanism, evidence only, exclude, and deferred. A mixed legacy file may have more than one disposition with named ranges or symbols.

States are seed, traced, translating, native-running, verified, replaced, excluded, or deferred. `traced` requires exact relevant symbols, callers, ordering, and attached evidence; a high-level file summary remains `seed`.

Record only translation hazards actually present in the current island inside the existing role or contract text: observable evaluation order and side effects; build-mode, macro, or compile-variant behavior; numeric conversion, truncation, overflow, or sentinel handling; index and capacity assumptions; and ownership, aliasing, reentrancy, or teardown order. Do not add empty checklist fields or create a project-wide lifetime inventory. When review finds a repeatable source-to-Swift error, record the concrete rule and audit previously translated rows for the same pattern.

`translating` includes code that only builds, links, or reaches an entry point. `native-running` requires the row's observable contract to execute through the applicable real shipping or creator path with focused green evidence. `verified` is the terminal state for translate, import-only, and evidence-only work after its source or reference comparison, deliberate differences, and applicable island evidence close. `replaced` requires the named platform replacement or superseding native mechanism to satisfy the row's observable contract with the same applicable evidence; naming a replacement is not enough. `excluded` requires approved reachability or scope evidence. `deferred` requires a named roadmap phase or missing dependency and proof that the deferred behavior is not required by the current claimed result.

Named ranges in a mixed-disposition file may share one state only while every range has reached that state. Split the row when their states diverge. An unresolved stub, stand-in constant, disabled production branch, reduced placeholder limit, or skipped test protecting the current contract cannot advance a row. A relevant empty body or no-op must be classified as implemented source behavior, an evidenced no-op, excluded, deferred, or unresolved; it is not automatically a defect. Any other nonexecuted test must be inapplicable under an approved terminal disposition with replacement or removal evidence; an explanation alone is insufficient.

At island closure, each range in the bounded census is `verified`, `replaced`, `excluded`, or explicitly `deferred`. A deferred range must name its roadmap phase and missing dependency and must not be required by the current observable contract. `seed`, `traced`, `translating`, and `native-running` cannot support a terminal claim for a current-census range. Historical broad rows may retain interim state for their still-open ranges, but a new closure claim splits out the completed range instead of hiding completed and deferred work under one umbrella state.

## Phase 1 level and acceptance-room record

This record was completed from full-build source plus a bounded run of the owned local retail Training data before import production began. The capture observed the complete reference `LoadLevel` result before `StartLevel`; it did not create a partial level or select a room during loading. Retail data, converted data, and the detailed capture remain local and ignored. The acceptance room narrows visual and editor proof; it does not cut the imported `Level`.

| Field | Recorded decision |
| --- | --- |
| Mission and level key | Full-build `Descent3/menu.cpp` identifies `Pilot Training` and loads `training.mn3`; `LoadMission` then exposes its sole level, `TrainingMission.d3l`, to `LoadMissionLevel` and `LoadLevel`. The project-owned canonical keys are mission `descent3.mission.pilot-training` and level `descent3.level.training-mission`. Filename spelling and case are import evidence, not canonical identity. |
| Complete-level evidence | The owned local `training.mn3` capture is 5,059,244 bytes with SHA-256 `fc1d81921cc4b2618e441b7b9d08c4bcb5cff90731be1bfa6f3a7b054fc0cb54`; its 39-entry HOG2 table places the 1,106,008-byte `TrainingMission.d3l` at offset 3,557,737 with SHA-256 `915a561cd3bd720d88bffed72fe41b4ff711c287711f060ecd9696e2cd5f7d41`. The unfiltered reference load produced 48 used rooms across source indices 0 through 50, 112 directed portal records, 40 used objects, 65,536 terrain cells, two paths, and one goal. The reference loader reported level checksum `6db74a2eb0c563de4eb11e6d4e91e59c`. These identify this owned capture; they do not promise that every retail release has identical bytes. |
| Acceptance-room identity | Source room index 3. The room has no authored name, so diagnostics and evidence call it `source-room-3`; the canonical model does not invent a stock-content name. |
| Selection reason | Room 3 is a small but substantive first island: 18 faces, two real portal boundaries, 17 faces with lightmap-info records, four referenced texture handles, and one placed `OBJ_POWERUP`. Its D3L record stores generic-table index 67 (`Invisiblepowerup`) and instance name `StartCourse`; `ReadObject` maps that through `generic_xlate` to reference-runtime object-info ID 68. Import must preserve the stored identity and perform the evidenced translation rather than reading raw ID 68, which names a different GNNM entry. The room exercises topology, material/lightmap data, and object representation without making the acceptance surface large. |
| Connected portal neighbors | Room 3 portal 0/face 0 connects to room 2 (`PortalRoom1`) portal 1/face 1 with `PF_RENDER_FACES`; room 2 portal 0/face 0 connects to room 1 portal 0/face 73 with the same flag. From the fixed room-3 portal-centered camera looking `+Y` through the accepted square 90-degree perspective, both forward faces are procedural rendered portals and source `RenderPastPortal` therefore traverses them. Their projected windows narrow from normalized `[-0.5, 0.5]` to `[-0.25, 0.25]`; room 1 has only the reciprocal portal, so depth/backtracking ends the source-visible closure at `3 → 2 → 1`. Room 3 portal 1/face 17 connects to room 4 but lies behind this camera and does not enter the view. Projection is an explicit camera value rather than an implicit renderer constant; a different aspect or field of view requires its own closure evidence. The complete resident `Level` still retains every room and reciprocal edge. |
| Dependency capture | With reference player ship index 0, the reference `PageInAllData` pass marked 232 unique texture handles, 115 unique sound handles, and 88 unique model handles; the loaded level held 2,531 used non-dynamic lightmap-info records. The fixed source-visible closure marks room-3 faces 0 through 16, room-2 faces 0, 2, 3, 4, and 5, and 103 traced room-1 faces: 125 face records with SHA-256 `564a9fd0b1264cbf19b124e55e36bd2ca1275dc86926093db538df1c97001002`. Its 123 per-face lightmap references hash to `e3e01bf4d0619be753ac4361f3829e780811497866b69bcb6de9da2deefd4456`; the 98 unique lightmap identities hash to `022c80403ef6d37064b74f11b38e441cbdbc9fef29c13c6ea0a68dfc53cfac76` and occupy pages 8, 13, and 19. The texture union is handles 698 `Lego Floor`, 793 `GreenArea`, 797 `LinewallHex`, 908 `Alien Force Field_1`, 985 `P-StorePlain3`, 1205 `PStore-CrossLight03`, 1331 `P-FacWarnStripes1S`, and 1332 `P-FacWarnStripes2S`. D3Import also resolves reached light-corona definitions to typed candidates and one shared canonical `StarFlare6.ogf` asset. The fixed camera emits no corona billboard after source near-cutoff and occlusion checks. Slice 3 retains that typed candidate and canonical asset for the later complete path, but free-camera corona drawing is not activated or claimed. The imported presentation closure is derived from marked faces and translated presentation paths, not room membership alone. The stored `StartCourse` object remains a later behavior dependency, not a presentation draw item. Runtime preparation consumes canonical identities and typed presentation values, never retail handles, packed pixels, or texture flags directly. |

Commit `73f3cce41871fb28d67a7fc0b467b55bad81d26e` archives the GPL-3.0-or-later evidence-only `tools/research/training_d3l_audit.rb`, `training_reference_runtime_trace.patch`, `training_reference_dependency_trace.patch`, and their `README.md` replay procedure. The parser mirrored only the released field order needed to audit the HOG table, D3LV-127 chunk walk, rooms, faces, portals, stored objects, paths, and goals. Its deterministic ignored output `runtime-data/revival-evidence/training-d3l-audit.txt` has SHA-256 `d20d6c34a5ac9f20eee3c500648d9a4514de668ad6af9984f7f8ce1e534e6ea4`. Those four tracked artifacts were deleted from the live checkout at their accepted gate before production import implementation. Their exact contents and reproduction procedure remain recoverable from the archived commit; no live document links to deleted paths.

The ignored `runtime-data/revival-evidence/training-room-runtime-trace.log` capture has SHA-256 `349e68ed340e8f18cd4a8b86193ea11b4b54b4dcffa38b648f154525f9b737dd`; its instrumented reference binary has SHA-256 `269cd2250124fed581938e651063db631cb1f0e5da885aded480496cc3ed4a44`. Both probes built and exited only as bounded historical research. No diagnostic patch is applied, and no retail artifact, converted asset, research flag, or second runtime path enters a product target.

## Phase 1 complete-level and acceptance-room seed

### Slice 1 HOG2 bounded preflight

The preflight inspected the active reader (`ReadHogHeader`, `ReadHogEntry`, `cf_OpenLibrary`, `cf_OpenFileInLibrary`, and `open_file_in_lib`), active CMake writer (`HogHeader`, `HogFileEntry`, `HogFormat::AddEntry`, `SortEntries`, and both stream operators), source tests and tracked fixture, creator-facing HOG editor load/sort/rewrite path, and obsolete legacy duplicate. The confirmed ledger gap was missing accounting for the active writer, source tests, creator path, and excluded duplicate; the rows below correct it. No linked unknown prevents the direct parser contract.

The checked boundary treats 68 as the absolute header size and table start, widens the `UInt32` header fields to Swift `Int` before computing `68 + count * 48` and ordered payload extents, and permits a first-payload offset after the table so header-to-payload padding remains source-compatible. It does not reproduce the C++ reader's fixed-width offset-overflow branches. A zero-entry archive is valid when its declared first-payload offset reaches EOF. The parser rejects short headers/tables/payloads, table overlap, payload offsets or extents beyond EOF, and unexplained bytes after the final payload. Names preserve exact spelling but must contain a NUL within the 36-byte field and contain only ASCII bytes before it; the active writer's exactly-36-byte unterminated output is deliberately rejected because all active and creator readers assume a C terminator. Folded ASCII keys must be strictly increasing, so out-of-order records and exact or case-folded duplicates/collisions fail instead of entering the source reader's ambiguous binary lookup.

#### Slice 1 implementation evidence

The production-contract red failed at compile time because `parseHOG2(_:)` did not exist, so testing was cancelled with 0 tests executed. The tracked source fixture then passed through the new production parser, and thirteen independently failing malformed-boundary contracts were each observed red before their production correction and then green. Three source-supported positive edge cases — zero entries, zero-length payloads, and header-to-payload padding — passed as characterizations of the generic parser path.

The final affected suite executed all 17 HOG2 tests with 0 failures and no skipped case. D3Import, RevivalMac, and RevivalEditor each built and linked in Debug and Release; every Release executable is arm64 with macOS 26.0 minimum and macOS 27.0 SDK load commands. Release D3Import prints only its usage contract and exits 64 when invoked without arguments, so the checked parser is deliberately not claimed as a complete shipping retail-processing path. The Slice 0+1 landing commit owns the exact red, green, suite, build, and entry-point commands and results.

### Slice 2 complete-Training bounded preflight

The packet owner inspected the exact owned `training.mn3` checkpoint and the released-source roots that determine its one-way import: the Training caller in `Descent3/menu.cpp`; mission selection and `MINE` parsing in `Descent3/Mission.{h,cpp}`; the mission-facing and mounted-HOG ranges of `cfile/cfile.cpp`; `Descent3/LoadLevel.{h,cpp}` including the D3LV-127 top-level walk, nested terrain chunks, rooms, faces, portals, objects, paths, goals, lightmap metadata, level information, and post-load ownership; the supporting `levelgoal`, room, terrain, object, path, and lightmap contracts; `gamesequence.cpp`'s completed `PageInAllData` walk; and editor open/save/play callers. It also checked the current Swift HOG2 parser, executable entry point, target composition, tracked synthetic fixture, ignored owned capture, ignored reference traces, and accepted content, world-loading, creator, testing, and verification contracts.

The bounded check found eight refinements to the provisional packet. D3Import must select `TrainingMission.d3l` through the sole mission `MINE` declaration rather than a filename-only archive search. Canonical topology must preserve sparse room indices plus stored texture, door, and generic page identity and reject lookup gaps instead of reproducing the source loader's fallback IDs. Placed objects retain the raw stored ID for every type, but only source definition-bearing generic and door types carry a resolved page definition or manifest dependency. `TERR` is a checked nested chunk stream. `NLMP` contributes canonical page and rectangle metadata plus room/object references now, while its validated pixel payload is reimported and prepared by Phase 1 Slice 3. `OHND` retains handle-generation continuity, and `INFO` retains the authored collision-ceiling flag as well as ordinary metadata. `NODE` is source-loaded navigation state, not a disposable derived cache; its checked payload remains deferred to the Phase 4 Training navigation/AI island. The nonempty `LIFE` payload is retained only by checked extent and hash as evidence for dormant future opportunity `F-001`; it has no current phase, runtime, authoring, or reimport owner, and adopting it requires new evidence plus an explicit accepted product amendment. Only `CNBS`, `CBOA`, and `AABB` are excluded derived caches here; `EDIT` is evidence for later native editor-document ownership rather than canonical legacy session state. A candidate native package must validate completely and its report must be fully written beside their destinations before publication. The same-directory sequence promotes the report first, restores the prior report on supported precommit cancellation or package-promotion failure, and makes the package rename the final commit. After that commit, only best-effort removal of the replaced package and report remains; cleanup failure warns but cannot turn a successful publication into failure. The covered cancellation and package-promotion failures leave the prior installed package and prior report in place. A report-restoration syscall failure is reported as the real OS error rather than hidden behind a claim of guaranteed rollback under arbitrary failure.

The exact checkpoint contains 21 unique top-level chunks in source order. The preflight stopped after every Training chunk had a named decode, canonical, deferred, or excluded disposition; the mission/profile selection path and shared package boundary were concrete; and the first focused red-test matrix could be written. No linked unknown blocks implementation, so `revival-wayfinding` is not invoked. At packet entry, `d3-content-import`, `revival-source-translation`, and `revival-verification` were aligned with the evidence then known and required no pre-production amendment. Closure review later amended `d3-content-import`, `revival-review`, and `revival-simplify` to encode the report-first/package-final commit contract and to require a supported production event, practical plausibility, and an accepted violated outcome before failure or recovery logic becomes a finding.

### Phase 2 Slice 6 object, model, and player-start presentation census

The Slice 6 fog-of-war preflight independently reconstructed this finite island from the released runtime and editor roots rather than treating the R1 routing map as a census. It corrected two proposed acceptance assumptions. Source-room-3 `StartCourse` is behind the fixed camera, but the source still admits its doubled-powerup-radius cube because every start-room object is portal-safe and its sphere crosses the near plane. Room-1 player ID 2 is rejected by the nested portal-window cube test even though a whole-frustum sphere test alone could admit it. The four source-enqueued object draws are therefore `StartCourse`, player ID 0, `UpGoal`, and `StartGoal`. `LeftGoal` and `ForwardGoal` also fail the room-1 portal-window test, and the co-located `OBJ_VIEWER` has no render type and is the viewer. The complete Training `Level` and all 40 imported object values remain resident; source admission of behind-eye `StartCourse` is not by itself a claim that it produces visible fragments.

### Phase 3 Slice 9 player-view prerequisite census

| Source formulas and ordering | Afterburn input replaces ordinary forward control with `afterburn * 1.6 * punch`; punch is `1.8` above 90-percent fuel, linearly falls from `1.8` to `1.0` strictly between 90 and 80 percent, and is `1.0` at both exact boundaries and below. The released Double subtraction and division narrow into Float intermediates before the Float punch, and the final multiplier promotes through Double before narrowing. Positive fuel drains by the previous frame duration and remains active on the frame that reaches zero; the next held frame clears. Released no-cooler recharge runs only on release with fuel below five and energy strictly above five, adds the previous frame duration capped to five fuel, and subtracts the untrimmed duration from energy. Wiggle adjusts falloff by half the previous duration toward one under thrust magnitude at least `0.1` and toward zero otherwise, uses `max(0.1, 1 - falloff)`, and applies amplitude times that scale times the delta of two legacy 16-bit `FixSin` samples from pre-update game time. Its up axis is sampled after direct mouselook mutates the object orientation and before ordinary rotational integration. Only `HIT_NONE` commits the point and returned room; contact refuses the whole displacement without response, and the later ordinary solver begins from the committed result. |

Direct values replace flags/globals, AppKit/GameController replace Win32/SDL tables, the existing collision radius replaces FVI's live-player scalar carrier, and the existing camera/projection carries the accepted FOV result. ### R1 maintained-source semantic-delta map

This is reviewed research evidence, not a Slice 6 source census, production disposition, or row-state transition. It makes the exact PiccuEngine and DESCENT 3MASTERED work discoverable so each owning island can begin from the diagnosed problem and corrected behavior instead of repeating the archaeology. The island still reconstructs its finite dependency set from the released-source roots, proves reachability, reads the exact maintained diff, and records any adopted correction in its normal row and deliberate-difference evidence.

| Evidence field | Record |
| --- | --- |
| Released baseline | DescentDevelopers `156cba8aafd997d27deb0902ba6026bcdcc1cfaf` remains the default shipped-behavior source. |
| Maintained pins | PiccuEngine `de4237abf67adc4eefded17bfcd09685ab311e87`; DESCENT 3MASTERED milestone 4 `52d85d0d40ba5235e4b26d9aedd358c15a15430e` and reviewed snapshot `c3e7bf99873f224b2d74f4e02e7b792bb9c35db3`. |
| Lineage | `56cab84f088e08bea8b69bf230e2923b9af6b508` is the latest commit in both pinned descendant DAGs; `61bb9a337d958be9b4716d67d548a06194ff2b5e` is an earlier shared-history anchor. The pinned DESCENT 3MASTERED snapshot contains 290 commits after the common commit and 174 after milestone 4; the pinned PiccuEngine snapshot contains seven after the common commit. |
| Provenance | Both descendant engine histories are GPL-3.0 evidence. Preserve the exact commit and affected source range for every translated formula or code-derived semantic. Bundled third-party code and assets retain separate licenses and rights; none enters Revival merely because it appears in a descendant. |
| Review | R1 source fidelity and senior scope review returned clean after correcting the common ancestor. Root review reproduced the ancestry and the seven Slice 6-entry diffs. Future islands re-inspect their mapped exact deltas before adoption; this block is a routing aid, not permanent parity or proof that every descendant commit matters. |

## Closure requirements for the Phase 1 level island

The level and acceptance-room identity record closed before Training import production began. The exact landed Phase 1 result is now indexed by the retrospective census above. Broad source files and later behaviors remain outside that finite claim in split deferred or provisional rows rather than being mislabeled complete.

1. expand this seed from the complete Training Level call and data trace;
2. add relevant headers only when they carry product semantics not already captured by their implementation owner;
3. name exact functions or ranges for every mixed-disposition file;
4. identify the complete level's eager working set and the acceptance room's renderer dependencies;
5. attach a focused reference capture, fixture, or source checkpoint to each translated contract;
6. record the planned native file or type only after the first red test makes that ownership concrete.

The exact Phase 1 census contains only `verified`, `replaced`, or `excluded` terminal anchors; its outward edges are deliberately `deferred` behind named later behavior. `seed`, `traced`, `translating`, and `native-running` remain interim states for broader ranges outside that claim and cannot be cited as Phase 1 closure evidence.
